# GRIPFACTORY LITE, CANON

**Version:** 0.30.237
**Status:** Active, Source of Truth
**Owner (final authority):** Dennis, sole ratifier
**Scribe:** persistent Claude session attached to the **gripfactoryPractical** project
**Home:** `canon/` in the repo at `Documents\Claude\Projects\gripfactory Practical` (canonical); the Claude Project docs are the Scribe's synced mirror

---

## 0. How to read this document

This file is the single source of truth for Gripfactory Lite. If a chat, a file, or a piece of code disagrees with this file, **this file wins** until it is amended through the Loop.

1. **Nothing is canon until it appears in this file.** Verbal agreements and prior chats do not count.
2. **Names listed here are exact:** no casing changes, no synonyms, no close enough.
3. **No drive-by edits.** Changes land through the Loop: decide, ratify, fold, version bump.

---

## 1. Identity

**Gripfactory Lite** (working title; repo folder "gripfactory Practical") is a lite rebuild of Gripfactory: the **opsHelpers database architecture** as the base, the **end state of Gripfactory as the design compass**, the **harvest console** incorporated. One job: **run harvest and post-harvest operations without interruptions.**

"C-Drive based" means the **repo lives on Dennis's disk**, outside corporate git (clarified 2026-08-27). The product itself is hosted.

It is also the **upstream canon for the node consoles.** Compliance Console canon already names "gripFactory canon, upstream and read-only" as its base: nodes may extend upstream canon and may never contradict it.

One codebase, two Cloudflare deployments, same behavior, different CSS tokens, each storing its own data on-site:

- **Grip internal site**: runs the actual 2026 harvest at Grip. Recipient: **Corey**.
- **C10 site**: the standalone product on The Continuum 10. Recipient: **Christine**.

## 1b. Terms (owner cleanup, 2026-08-31, verbatim where quoted)

- **HBN**: "a METRC name for a harvest, no matter what parent packages come and go, this HBN follows every subsequent package around." The HBN is the METRC harvest NAME; it rides the HarvestName column of METRC uploads and names upload files.
- **Lot**: "The year and sequential order of the harvest YY###."
- **Package ID**: "METRC Package ID, can refer to all of or parts of a lot."
- Lifecycle: "Early in life, product is managed by lot. As the lots get broken down, the lot number matters less and less. But at the end of everything when you're reviewing the data, the HBN is the only thing that ties a FG to harvest batch."
- Known tension, unruled: the app's operator surfaces say "Start lot / Finish lot" for wet_weight_entries rows (one per destination within an HBN), while the canonical Lot is the YY### itself. Renaming those surfaces awaits an owner ruling.

## 2. Success criteria

1. The Grip harvest runs on it from **2026-09-01** without interruption.
2. A **hosting package** ships to both recipients: a **zip of the repo plus a README addressed to the recipient's Claude agent**, which deploys it to that recipient's Cloudflare (Corey: Grip internal; Christine: C10). (The Harvest Console `handoff/` pattern is the proven precedent.)
3. Related hard date: the C10 Compliance Console surface has a hard need of **2026-09-09** (its own canon, §2).

## 3. Roles and the Loop (Tier 1)

| Role | Who | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| Owner | Dennis | Final authority; sole ratifier of canon |
| Scribe | this session | Canon, the process map, Cursor prompts, design-agent engagements; writes no code |
| Builder | Claude Code (ruled 2026-08-27: "switch from cursor to code") | Code agents deployed by the Scribe in its workspace for autonomous slices; Dennis's own Claude Code sessions (voice-driven) for interactive work, fed by Scribe-written briefs in prompts/. Cursor remains a fallback lane. |
| Design agents | independent agents | Design assistance, engaged by the Scribe |
| Recipients | Corey (Grip ops), Christine (C10) | Receive the package; testers, not approvers |

**The Loop:** Dennis states the goal; the Scribe grounds against canon, then directs the work; the Scribe surfaces results and proposed amendments; Dennis ratifies, overrides, or defers; the Scribe folds, bumps the version, updates MEMORY and _NOW.

**Rulings interface (ratified 2026-08-27):** decisions are put to Dennis through AskUserQuestion pickers, never as lists stranded in chat. Durable state lives in canon files, not in the conversation.

## 4. Standing rules (guardrails)

- **G-1, One remote, GitHub, private (amended 2026-09-24, canon 0.30.223; owner: "Let's get this thing on github while it's somewhat functional").** The repo on Dennis's disk is the working tree and the source of truth; it has one remote, a private GitHub repository under his account or the OCG organization, pushed by his hands alone. The Scribe never pushes, never adds a remote, never runs an index-touching git command in the mount. For now `main` mirrors the disk (his pick: "Push main only for now"); building on branches waits for a later ruling. DROPS/, db_backups/, DEPLOY/, _to_delete/ and RESEARCH/ never ride (.gitignore). Before this amendment: No GitHub, no pushes, no remotes (2026-08-27).
- **G-2, Build lanes (amended 2026-08-27: "the bones all seem to work").** The Scribe may apply small slices directly from its session; Cursor remains the lane for large or structural work. Either lane owes the same discipline: sealed final check, session report, migrations frozen post-deploy.
- **G-3, No flubdubs.** No speculative features, invented fields, or decorative complexity. The screens fill with real operations; lean beats clever. (Dennis, 2026-08-27)
- **G-4, Repo on disk, app in the cloud (amended 2026-08-27).** The repo lives on Dennis's disk, never corporate git. The app runs as two Cloudflare sites (Grip internal, C10): same code and behavior, different CSS tokens, each storing its own data on-site. SQL stays Postgres-portable for the possible future Plato-on-Aurora integration.
- **G-5, opsHelpers is the db base**, defined as Plato Bible §4 standing rules, §5 naming conventions, and §6 canonical schema: snake_case English identifiers, grams canonical, UTC storage, audit columns on every row, soft delete plus edit-with-reason into `record_change_log`, lookup-table enums, configurable utilities as data not code, repository-layer storage abstraction. The Plato governance apparatus is **not** carried.
- **G-6, Canon home and mirror.** `canon/` in the repo is the canonical home; the Claude Project holds the Scribe's synced mirror. Every fold updates both. Repo home ratified at `Documents\Claude\Projects\gripfactory Practical` (2026-08-27).
- **G-7, The Box Doctrine is the build law.** See §5.
- **G-8, Style law: no em dashes or en dashes anywhere**: chat, documents, UI strings, code comments, generated files. Commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or a plain spaced hyphen instead. (Owner directive, global, 2026-08-11.)
- **G-9, Ship clean.** Shipped packages carry no personnel or internal-meeting content. (Harvest Console v0.94 scrub is precedent; crew names in working data stay internal.)
- **G-10, Build verification.** Every Cursor prompt requires a session report plus the sealed final check: byte size, sha256, parse test, and tail inspection for every touched file, output pasted verbatim into the report.

## 5. The Box Doctrine (ratified 2026-08-27)

The unit of build is **the box**. The harvest process map (Dennis's Visio, transcribed in `canon/PROCESS_MAP.md`, image at `canon/harvest-process-map.png`) is the territory. **The map is walked in order.**

**The Box Contract**, all three or it is not done:

1. A **worker** can perform the process.
2. A **manager** can audit it.
3. A **report or CSV** comes out of it.

The working method per box:

1. Dennis supplies the current tool: the OpsHelpers equivalent or the spreadsheet the box runs on today.
2. The Scribe and Dennis **talk the box through before any design.** No designing ahead of the current box.
3. Then spec, then Cursor builds, then verification, then the box is adopted and the walk moves on.

**Standing duty:** the Scribe holds the map and reminds Dennis when work drifts off the current box. The Scribe polices itself the same way: design sprawl is drift.

## 6. Architecture and key decisions

- **ADR-1 (2026-08-27):** opsHelpers db architecture per G-5 is the foundation. Proven in operation; a 5-day runway forbids novel architecture.
- **ADR-2 (2026-08-27, amended same day):** **TypeScript** backbone per the cross-project standard (Dennis 2026-07-22; SalesHelper canon §2). Store of record on Cloudflare is **D1**: a documented exception to the Postgres law, accepted by owner delegation (2026-08-27: limited resources, no appetite for another server). All SQL is written ANSI-portable so a Postgres/Aurora port is a dialect change, not a rewrite. PGlite remains the option for a future local edition.
- **ADR-3 (2026-08-27):** Gripfactory end state is the design compass; the harvest console is incorporated, not reinvented. The new-farm edition is the live console lineage (promoted 2026-08-06); the exact incorporation shape gets decided when the walk reaches console-shaped boxes.
- **ADR-4 (2026-08-27):** Single-file static deliverables for the C10 host contract (one self-contained HTML file, no server, no network calls) apply to console-type nodes hosted on the existing C10 static path; the Gripfactory Lite app itself deploys per ADR-5.
- **ADR-5 (2026-08-27):** **The queue spine.** The build follows Dennis's nine-step chain (Acquire HBN → HBN Queue → Wet Weight Entry → Dry Space Queue → Trim Packages Entry → Cryo Queue → Cryo Weight Entry → Testing Queue → manual testing-queue controls) as simple forms on **Cloudflare Workers + D1**, two wrangler environments (grip, c10), stations and queues defined in one typed registry so pipeline order is data and new stations insert without rework. HBN date segment: **YYDDD Julian** (owner ruling 2026-08-27), replacing the 25H Excel serial.
- **ADR-6 (2026-08-27):** **The mint law, corrected against the 25H formula and owner ruling.** `<YYDDD>-<farm>-<grow_code>-<strain_code>-<manicure>-<silo>-<YY###>`. The trailing segment is **YY + a 3-digit year-wide lot serial** in mint order (26001, 26002, ...), resetting each year: the floor's primary identifier and the largest number on the traveler. Numeric silo numbers render 2-digit zero-padded (5 → 05); blank → NA. Serials are never reused; voids leave auditable gaps.

- **ADR-7 (2026-08-31): The auth surface.** Three layers, all live. (1) **Cloudflare Access** walls the whole site: Zero Trust org gflite, One-time PIN login, emails ending @gripcannabis.com. (2) **Identity is the Access login email** (owner ruling: "Replace the Person selector with the cloudflare email address used at login"): a middleware reads Cf-Access-Authenticated-User-Email, people rows auto-create on first login (unique email column, display name from the local part, editable in Lookups), the actor for every write is the login person and is never trusted from the form. The wet weight **Lead stays a picker** (owner ratification) and stores on the lot as lead_person_id. Local dev and smoke run without Access and act as dev@local. Access-JWT verification (AUD tag) is parked hardening. (3) **PIN gate** (owner ruling: "Gate Admin and Interco logistics with a PIN prompt, 21319 is the only valid PIN"): /admin, /admin/*, and /interco demand the PIN, which lives as data in app_config (L-4), one unlock holds per device for 12 hours via cookie. Amended 2026-08-31: /fulfillment and /fulfillment/* sit behind the same gate (the Fulfillment tile, folded at 0.7.1).

- **ADR-8 (2026-08-31): The two-pane station theme is law.** Owner, verbatim: "left pane: available-in-queue, right pane: what's currently saved about that record and the ability to add more to it"; extended by owner ruling to everything ("It will extend to everything"). Every station renders its feeding queue as the left pane and the picked record's saved state plus its add controls as the right pane; record views (the wet lot ledger, the unload dry ledger) render as right-pane panels on the two-pane page, never as standalone pages. Acquire HBN is the source station with no feeding queue and keeps its plain form: the sole standing exception until the owner says otherwise.

**Reference map** (read-only sources, sibling folders): `gripfactory/PLATO_BIBLE.md` (the opsHelpers schema and rules) · `gripfactory/SCRIBE_NOTES_V4.md` (verification discipline, failure patterns) · `Harvest Console/harvest-console-CANON.md` and `Harvest Console/new-farm/NEW-FARM-CANON.md` (console lineage) · `Harvest Console/handoff/HOSTING.md` (hosting package precedent) · `SalesHelper/CANON.md` (stack law, style law) · `Compliance Console/ComplianceConsole_CANON.md` (node contract, C10/GF split).

## 7. Lessons register (the compass, ratified 2026-08-27)

- **L-1** Ceremony dies, schema survives. Plato's Tier 3 governance was retired within weeks; its schema and naming rules outlived everything. Govern light, keep the schema strict.
- **L-2** Cloud lanes go dormant, offline-first wins. v4_v2 on Cloudflare stalled; the single-file offline console shipped and got hosted. (History stands; the 2026-08-27 hosting ruling reverses course deliberately, hedged by the zip + README package and Postgres-portable SQL.)
- **L-3** The proven build loop: Scribe writes the spec, Cursor builds, headless verification, owner sees it working. Honor it per box.
- **L-4** Values live in data, not code. The 448 g/lb buried-formula lesson (Plato §4.10): any raw-to-displayed transformation is user-editable configuration.
- **L-5** Truncation is real. Every write gets the sealed final check; mount views can lie; sha256 from both sides settles it.
- **L-6** OneDrive and folder mounts bite: a live .git will not run on the mounted folders from the Scribe's side; Cursor's native git is fine; repos travel as zips.

## 9. Open items / parking lot

1. **Slice 2 remainder**: the spear field is built (slice 2a); the B05/B10 deepening talk-through remains (who weighs where, manifest shape, hang vs silo differences).
1b. **26H workbook edit owed** (Dennis/team, one cell): Abbr tab, Blue Andeez strain code 071 → 078, so the workbook and the app agree.
2. **Cloudflare Access on the Grip site: DONE 2026-08-31** (Zero Trust org gflite, All traffic, One-time PIN, emails ending @gripcannabis.com). Remaining: exposure model for the C10 site; optional rename of the auto-generated org label (aged-snowflake-24fc) in Zero Trust settings.
2b. **Post-break-in reset**: Dennis and the Scribe will break the deployed site in on test data; before the first real mint, the grip D1 gets wiped clean (drop and recreate the database, reapply migrations, swap the new id into wrangler.toml, redeploy).
3. **Theme tokens**: real Grip and C10 CSS token sets (C10 tokens already extracted in Harvest Console). **The Grip look landed 2026-08-31: prompts/CERBERUSTHEMEPROMPT.md (Cerberus: steel and gold, honeycomb, boot scrawl), owner-queued "for after all these build changes".**
3b. **Inventory transfer station** (owner, 2026-08-31: "which we will need to build out"): a future weight-recording station under the balance law; awaits its box in the walk.
4. **B02 leftovers**: ABC column meaning; when Cuts and Plants land; audit and report shape beyond CSV.
5. **Console incorporation shape**: decided when the walk reaches console-shaped boxes.
6. **Real product name**: "Gripfactory Lite" is a working title.
7. **9/1 day-one minimum**: which boxes must be live falls out of the walk.
8. **Lessons register additions** in Dennis's own words, whenever he wants to add them.
9. **Fulfillment node follow-ups**: a menu.json refresh lane for our hosted copy (today it serves the static 2026-08-11 menu; the form's data-age badge shows the honest age); /api/send order capture (unrouted today, form falls back to download plus mailto); whether Corey's grip-saleshelper.pages.dev copy stays live alongside ours.
10. **Unload travelers and the METRC Package ID (owner-parked, 2026-08-31):** whether each dry weight can opt to print a traveler, and modeling the package flow (traveler = Package ID + container weight + x of n, reduced to a dry total of a package ID). Waits on the owner: "I need to get the weights where they need to be before I answer that completely."

| 2026-09-04 | **Crew feedback via the owner (verbatim): "HBN Add market strain textbox (this will override the product's name in subsequent packages, but not determine the HBN (this is what we do if say we have a bunch of candy strains, we might call half of it 'rainbow candies' and the other half 'yummy candies', but future Dennis will still want to know what the actual cultivar was. Start time and silo are optional fields and they're planned fields, not data fields."** THE MARKET STRAIN LAW: a free-text market_strain on Acquire HBN (migration 0026); when set it OVERRIDES the product display name on everything downstream of the batch (minted inventory items, manifests' ItemName, listings, labels, external testing rows) while the HBN token, the strain lookup linkage, and the audit trail keep the true cultivar; blank means the cultivar displays as ever. It matches the Market Strain column already living in the owner's potency workbook. THE PLANNED FIELDS LAW: Start Time and Silo # are optional PLANNED fields, never data fields: labels say planned and optional, empty always accepted, and the start-time help stops claiming to anchor TATs (TATs anchor on entry and status dates, which are data). THE OWNER'S ADDENDUM, moments later, verbatim: "Actual start time and silo is determined at wet weight entry, which incidentally is what ratifies the HBN." So the model completes: Acquire carries the plan (planned start time, planned_dryspc), Wet Weight captures the actuals (actual_dryspc already lives there; the lot header gains an actual start time, a DATA field, 24-hour law, optional so the floor is never blocked), and the HBN RATIFIES at wet weight entry, which the recall pane already embodies (weightless HBNs are the recallable unratified ones). THE THIRD RULING OF THE SITTING, verbatim: "the HBN can't be the queue item for wet weights, it needs to be simpler. 1.(a sequence number) Farm/Strain/Date is all that's needed. HBN is inherited by both processes on the CSV." THE WET QUEUE ITEM LAW: the wet station's left-pane rows render a sequence number then Farm/Strain/Date (the TRUE cultivar, not the market name: the market override governs subsequent packages, and a wet queue row is not yet a package); the picked heading takes the same simple form with the full HBN muted beneath (the unload idiom); the HBN itself keeps riding the record, the panels, and every CSV untouched. THE FOURTH REPORT OF THE SITTING, a live-floor bug (owner, verbatim): "I just added a wet harvest of after party at Arlington, but there's no inbound record of it. The header should say 'truck 1'." Scribe reading to verify by reproduction: a finished wet truck lot rides status in_transit, but the Interco menu rework left the intra-farm truck queue with no crew-visible surface (the old Inbound Queue lived in Admin Data Queues), and the crew's mental model is that a truck on the road IS inbound; the fix surfaces in-transit wet truck lots on /interco/receiving as their own Inbound trucks section, each line headed Truck N with farm, strain, date, and weight, linking to the lot where the TL reassigns Move To on arrival (the reasoned pencil is the assign-a-silo-on-the-fly of the held 0.16.1 reading). MORE WALK FEEDBACK, same sitting, verbatim: "in compliance console, visually distinguish the metrics from the buttons, maybe put the metrics in their own pane and adjust the colors slightly" (BUILT into this slice: the four KPI tiles ride a tinted kpi-pane labeled Metrics, apart from the menu cards). "In Marketplace, the package tag field is referring to a human readable label, not a package tag" / "Add a category field to the listings page and a config in Admin for the list it generates" / "qty lbs should be a calculated field. The person adding a listing is going to be using grams. Either the flow is off, or the label needs to be changed. We also need to be able to split packages in this module which will make the package field a must-have for the internal team (it's practically a throwaway for the sales team)" (LOGGED; the marketplace rework, package splits included, is the NEXT dispatched slice: splits intersect the held trim-split law and need their own box). THE ONE-EXPORT-HOME LAW, verbatim: "Remove export manifest from the virtual moves list. it needs its own place in the admin list instead. I'm worried that if it's in both places, a user can easily and absent mindedly try and run it in the moment than again at the end of the day. The exports into metrc being in one spot forces us to come up with a process for them." BUILT into this slice as the general principle: every METRC-bound CSV surfaces for download and mark-uploaded in EXACTLY ONE place, /admin/uploads; manifests (interco and virtual) pend there as metrc_uploads rows keyed by movement, and the per-move export buttons leave the Virtual Moves log, the outbound finished list, and every other scattered spot. THE TRIM REPORT, same walk, verbatim (with screenshots: Gelonade 26025, Original 275,000.00, Recorded 285,001.00, Remaining -10,001.00): "This exchange occurs in the trim sheet just like in the weight stations. Needs a plus sign, etc instead of the 'Add Package' button the top line should be blank by default, plus adds the record to the below list and the list has edit and delete options. Also, I over issued, no warning and the package is still in the trim queue." BUILT into this slice: the trim entry takes the [+] rapid-add idiom (one blank top line, the plus commits the typed package to the list beneath and clears the boxes, the list rows carry the reasoned pencil and x, Save stays), and the live overissue confirm rides the plus exactly like unload (warn on crossing the parent remaining, never block) with a standing OVER ISSUED banner whenever a picked balance runs negative. (6) VIRTUAL MOVE TO PROCESSOR: a virtual Michigan manifest between the license ending 832 and the license beginning AU-P; left pane all items in 832 (the dry landing spot plus the grow); right pane all items in the processor with their local inventory locations; the standard 832-to-processor move is CSA to CSB; the reverse is more complicated but always lands in CSA (fed by many). OWNER, VERBATIM: "We need to strike the menu and take a look at what actually happens in a typical harvest, then back in the exceptions.  Somewhere, we've gotten wires crossed.  I don't want to change the surfaces unless necessary, but I need to give you the sequence of events for most of the logistics process: 1. Acquire HBN 2. Wet weights are collected by TLs 3. Wet weights + HBN + Plant Tags + Barn(at whatever license the plant tags are at) + New Package Tag ID = METRC Harvest CSV 4. If the harvest was on a med license at any farm, the new package ID is put on a manifest to that farm's AU license, then virtually received in 'Barn'.  If it's on an AU license already, the new Package ID is created in 'Barn'. 5. If this is a harvest at ARL or PNS, the new package ID is added to a manifest to deliver to PNN/832 'Barn' - note: 832 is an AU license, so we make sure the equivalent transfer is done beforehand. 6. When the truck arrives, it's received into PNN/832/Barn in METRC. 7. Whether the lot came from North or was shipped there, the wet weight begins its life in 832 Barn.  It gets transferred to 832 Silo #n immediately upon receipt/harvest tag creation." And: "Try not to conflate terms here.  This is what happens to the material in real life, but I'm also describing the METRC processes that need to occur at the same time.  Keep the same methodology on the working panes, but consider the flow of data in our process, and really stretch here." (R2) "Start time in Load Silo needs the time input mask". (R3) "The "LOADED" time is when the clock should start". (R4) "Null Market Strains should automatically inherit the real strain, the market strain should be what's listed from Trim Packages entry forward (The picture should say "[Last 6 of Tag Number]/26001/TEST MOOKY/250,000(drop the decimals)." with the Trim Packages Entry screenshot reading "26001 / Bermuda Triangle / 250,000.00" in the Unloaded Queue and the heading. (R7) "Cryo and Trim need the dates saved to each record, along with the hand entry of the operator filling in the data". SCRIBE READINGS. R1: the road (and the Arrivals left pane) read two sources, in-transit truck lots and completed manifests with an open item; since the spine (0.30.2) every truck lot cuts a manifest at finish, and the floor's WET-lot manifests put the same lot in transit, so ONE truck yields TWO rows, the second labeled origin - destination - short movement id and nothing else, which reads as an orphan; by the screenshot 589af120 is TEST MOOKY's own manifest (ARL to PNN, the only truck on the road). Two real defects sit behind it: the manifest-pane receive (Receive all, Finish Receipt) moves the package but never flips the lot, stranding a truck row in transit; Finish Receipt skips WET items entirely, so a WET-lot manifest never closes. LAW: THE TRUCK CARRIES ITS PAPER: a manifest riding an in-transit truck lot (born package or WET item) shows nowhere on its own; the truck row carries the manifest number; the one-button receive closes the manifest whichever way the lot rode; the manifest pane, if reached by URL, receives the lot too (status to drying, the fallback mint for a pre-ledger lot); every other road chip names its contents (package, strain, grams), never a bare id; legacy orphans heal from the UI (a stranded truck row takes the one-button; a WET-item manifest closes at Finish Receipt). R2/R3: the Start time takes the app's HH:MM colon mask (data-time24-mask, the Acquire idiom); THE LOADED TIME IS THE CLOCK'S ZERO: the typed Start time (resolved in the browser's local day; blank = now) becomes loaded_at and the clock runs from it the moment the silo loads; the load view shows Loaded (rendered local) and the clock; Start stays as ruled and is greyed from the load; Pause/Resume and the Pencil (editing loaded_at, the zero) unchanged. R4: LAW: a blank market strain inherits the strain name at Acquire (stored on the batch; 0036 backfills the blanks); FROM TRIM PACKAGES ENTRY FORWARD the strain shown is the MARKET strain, and the lot reads last-6-of-tag / serial / market strain / grams with no decimals (his picture; a blank tag drops its token; the app's spaced slash stays). Before Trim (wet, dry, unload) nothing changes. R5: the Trim working pane lists the lot's entered packages beneath the entry form, newest first, each with the pencil (reasoned edit) and the x (reasoned void), both behind the PIN (/record/wip_packages/* joins the PIN gate). R6: LAW: at Trim, when Recorded reaches or passes Original (Remaining at or below zero) the pane prompts REMAINING 0 (naming the overage when there is one) and offers [Remove from queue]: the lot trims (leaves the Unloaded Queue) and, when over, the adjustment CSV pends at the export home (fork 7's corrective lane, papered automatically; the flash says so); the under gate (short of the parent) is unchanged. R7: LAW: every trim package stores its trim date and the hand-typed operator; every cryo weight stores its cryo date and operator (0036: wip_packages trim_date, trim_operator, cryo_date, cryo_operator); the forms carry Date (defaults today) and Operator (typed) above the rows; the lists show them. Worker + migration 0036 (append-only; data backfill for market strains). |

SESSION_REPORTS/2026-09-08_mount_sos_pick_orders.md, migration 0038 (so_mounts, mounted_sales_orders keyed by Zoho's SalesOrder Number, mounted_sales_order_lines keyed by seq inside the order), src/fulfillment.ts. BUILT ON GRIPFACTORISH'S BEHAVIOR: [Mount SOs] takes the Zoho Inventory Sales Order export (csv) at /fulfillment/mount-sos (known by a Product Sub or Status (Internal) column or a sales-order filename; anything else refuses in words); each line buckets by Item.CF.Product Sub through ZOHO_SUB_MAP in its verbatim order (thca, bulk flower, prepack ounces / max out bags, vape / cart, infused pre-rolls, infused flower, concentrate, shake, trim); crif and processing lines are ignored and counted; an unmapped sub still mounts as a line to pick; one order per SalesOrder Number, lines in file order. THE MOUNT IS DURABLE AND IDEMPOTENT: an order upserts by so_number (Zoho's fields refresh, our pick fields stay); lines match by seq and keep picked_qty, short_flag, pick_note; a dropped line soft-deletes ("dropped by re-mount"); a voided order the export still carries revives with a log line; an order absent from a later export keeps its last state and is chipped "not in last mount". Every mount lands a so_mounts row. Nothing writes back to Zoho. THE READOUTS ride the Mount SOs page: the Sales Pipeline (Shipping Today, Accepted, Submitted, Picking, Fulfilled, Shipped by the ten categories BUD TRIM BIO PREPACK PREROLLS SHAKE DISTILLATE INFUSED BUD VAPES THCA with CAT_UOM lb/ea/L; distinct orders per category, TOTAL distinct across categories; quantity per unit, Shipped reads QuantityInvoiced, TOTAL quantity lb-only), the SO Status Detail, the open orders with Pick links. EFFECTIVE STATUS = our pick_status (picking, fulfilled) over Zoho's CF.Status (Internal) lowercased (submitted, approved, shipped): gripfactorish's two grayed rows fill from the pick. SHIPPING TODAY = Expected Shipment Date equals the BROWSER'S day (data-today on the page; a differing device date reloads once with ?today=YYYY-MM-DD, isIsoDate-guarded; the worker's UTC day is the fallback). [Pick Orders] at /fulfillment/pick-orders is two panes by the station law: left, every live order not shipped; right, the picked order (header with licenses origin to destination, Zoho's two statuses, our pick record in browser-rendered time; [Print pick sheet]) and its lines in WALKING ORDER (rack addresses by prefix, row, bay, shelf; then named spots alphabetically; blanks last; grouped under location rows). Unstarted: [Start pick] with Picked by over the sign-off names (0.30.12; a name off the list refuses; a second start refuses). Picking: picked quantity, short checkbox and note per line, [Save progress] under the absent-field law of 0.30.10 (a line whose picked_ field is not posted is unchanged; the picked_ field is the checkbox's witness), [Complete pick] with Fulfilled by. Completing marks the order fulfilled HERE with fulfiller and time; a line picked under its ordered quantity is FLAGGED SHORT, NEVER BLOCKED; an unmarked line stays as the paper shows it. Fulfilled: read-only with the marks. THE PICK SHEET at /fulfillment/pick-orders/:id/print is a standalone letter print document (customer, ship date, sales person, licenses, delivery; lines by location with checkbox, tag, ordered, unit, picked with short in bold; Picked by and Fulfilled by sign lines), the paper that replaces the picklist hand written on the invoice. Reasons so_mount, pick_start, pick_save, pick_complete; every start, save and complete logs. The two fulfillment tiles lose their orange barrels; fulfillmentReservedPage retires. THE EIGHT FORKS OF 0.30.17, RESOLVED BY SCRIBE DEFAULT (the owner overrules by a word): 1 Zoho status stays internal, nothing writes back; 2 pick location = Item.CF.Location Code; 3 completing = status and paper only, no manifest (FG not in the ledger yet); 4 shortages flag, never block; 5 sign-off names sign (picker at start, fulfiller at complete); 6 paper = the letter pick sheet, no label; 7 the marketplace node's sales_orders untouched; 8 the readouts live on Mount SOs. TWO MORE NAMED: lines match by seq (the export carries no line id); the three tables are off the record routes (no pencil or x on a mounted order yet), so voiding a stale order is a fork left open. | 0.30.36 | 2026-09-09 | MOUNT ZOHO INVENTORY ONTO THE MARKETPLACE (owner question, verbatim: "Is there a place in the app that I can drop current Zoho inventory and it will update the offerings on the marketplace?"; the Scribe answered no and put four forks; the owner answered with the real Items export attached, verbatim: 1 "attached"; 2 "If zoho is using  each, it's weight, if it's oz or g, show that as "market-facing UOM" and actual weight in grams - Lbs = 448 grams"; 3 "Zoho should show a SKU, that's usually the METRC package ID"; 4 "Not sure what you mean, picking and shipping will remove it.  Dropping a new inventory should correct it"). BUILT Scribe-direct (worker + migration 0039): a ZOHO tab on the Marketplace (PINned with the node) with the drop form, the last mount's summary and the offerings grouped by Product Sub. THE OFFERING LAW: the export is the truth; every Active inventory item with stock on hand becomes a Zoho-sourced listing keyed by SKU (Item ID, then name, when missing), two bins of one SKU fold into one offering, a newer export updates in place, lists what has stock, unlists what is gone or at zero; hand-entered listings untouched; nothing writes back to Zoho. THE UOM LAW: market-facing UOM is Zoho's unit (lb, oz, g, each, L) in any spelling, a blank unit read from the category (Prepack Ounces, Max Out Bags, Vape/Cart, Infused Pre-Rolls, Infused Flower are eaches; other blanks are pounds); grams = lb x 448, oz x 28, g, each x the size of one (name first: "1.2g", "15g", "1oz"; else category: Prepack Ounce 28 g, Max Out Bag 2.5 oz = 70 g, its price exactly 2.5x the ounce prepack); liters and unknown eaches read weight unknown. THE SKU LAW: a METRC-shaped SKU (1A + 22 hex) is the listing's package tag; the ledger's matching applies: Matching, Not matching, or Zoho only when the ledger never saw the tag (100 branded text SKUs are Zoho only by nature); a 0 g row never matches for free. SCRIBE DEFAULT, FLAGGED: stock on hand of a billion or more is a placeholder (his export carries eight catch-all items at 1e12 units); counted as placeholders skipped, never offered; PLACEHOLDER_STOCK is one word. The order pick offers Zoho rows grouped by category; the Listings pane counts them and keeps its table hand-entered. Migration 0039: listings gain source, sku, zoho_item_id, market_uom, market_qty, each_grams, price, zoho_location, thc_pct, zoho_status, zoho_mount_id, zoho_mounted_at (index source, sku); zoho_inventory_mounts records each drop; every write logged with reason zoho inventory mounted, batched in chunks of 400. OWNER, VERBATIM, with eight METRC exports attached (seven plants files, one 832 packages file; never vendored): "Let's start the internal inventory slice. It would be better if it asked how many plants were harvested after the you finish entering wet weights." (2) "Manifest CSV export uses HBN where package tag belongs." (3) "In the trim room, if package code means tag #, please use tag number instead of package code, that will confuse the team. If package code does not mean tag #, what does it mean?" (4) "Shake is not an option in the drop-down menu in the trim room ." (5) "In the trim room, if you enter all your entries = all the source package weight (if the wip package = 1,000 pounds and all the trim weights = 1,000 pounds) you get a pop up warning that you have entered more weight than is in wip." (6) "For wet weights, dry weights, and trim, you enter 1 tote weight at a time. In Cryo you enter one weight per bud wip. I think there should be two entries for cryo, new package tag and weight, so that you are entering each packages tag # and 22679g (or whatever the weight is) for each final package. THE METRC MIRROR (0042): src/metrc.ts reads METRC's xlsx as exported (central-directory zip walk, DecompressionStream deflate-raw, the sheet streamed row by row, shared strings, booleans, dates as serials under the styles' date formats, blanks) or the CSV of the same grid, by the bytes; the file name gives license and grid (MetrcMichigan<AUGC|AUP|GRC><6 digits><Plants|Packages><grid>), the header gives the kind, the form names license and phase when the name does not. THE MIRROR LAW: the export is the truth of METRC as of the drop for its license (and phase for plants): unseen tags add, moved or returned or re-phased rows update in place, rows the file no longer carries are marked gone (only in the drop's own phase); multi-row inserts under D1's 100 bound parameters (6 plants or 3 packages a statement), chunks of 400, the mount row leads the batch; one log row per mount, never per plant. Tables metrc_mounts, metrc_plants (ten METRC columns plus license, phase, mount, first seen, changed, gone), metrc_packages (twenty-two plus the same), internal_field_values keyed by tag; the registry is lookup category internal_field (Value the key, Label the name, Tone = plants, packages, or blank for both) in Admin, Data Lookups, Inventory, Internal fields; seeded (F3, his to amend): Location code, Quality grade, Market strain, Zoho SKU, Intended lane, Notes. | 0.30.42 | 2026-09-11 | THE SLICED MOUNT (a live incident, built Scribe-direct, worker only, no migration, the fast lane). THE OWNER'S SCREENSHOT, verbatim: Cloudflare Error 1102 "Worker exceeded resource limits", Ray ID a39a93183d34d8ab, 2026-09-11 23:50:27 UTC, gripfactory-lite-grip.gflite.workers.dev; his words with it: "On the third export mounting". THE READING: one request read, shaped, diffed and wrote a whole plants export and held it all at once; the small files pass, a plants export of tens of thousands of rows hits the Worker's ceiling (128 MB, and one invocation's CPU); the first two mounts stand; a third that landed partly reconciles on the re-drop by THE MIRROR LAW. THE LAW: the drop page sends the file in rounds of 5,000 rows (METRC_SLICE_ROWS), each round its own request (slice, slice_size, mount_id), each answering JSON, refusals in JSON words; the file streams once per round (every row read so the tags seen and the summary total the whole file; only the window's rows shaped, diffed by tag against the mirror, and written); round zero writes the mount row with the file's row count and a summary reading mounting with rows done; every round adds its counts to the mount row in the same final batch as its rows; the last round marks gone (the mirror's live tags under the license, and the phase for plants, minus every tag the file carries), writes the real summary and logs the final counts; a later round is checked against its mount (kind, license, phase, row count) or refused; a mount whose rounds never finished reads unfinished, N of total rows in the Mounts table; the line under the button counts the rounds; a browser without the script posts once and the Worker walks the file in one request as before. THE MIRROR LAW ACROSS OUR OWN LICENSES (a fault found on the way): the window's existing rows are read by tag alone, so a package the mirror holds under one license, dropped in another license's file, updates in place, license included, where the 0.30.38 mount would have collided on the tag. MEASURED on his exports: one round of the 48,149-row GR-C-001132 flowering file about 1.0 s and 18 MB of heap in Node; in the browser against wrangler dev the 34,445-row GR-C-001405 file mounted in 7 rounds in 6.2 s and the 48,149-row file in 10 rounds in 11.4 s, every round 200. Gates: tsc 0, vitest 22 files 542 tests, dash 0, from-zero smoke SMOKE PASS at 43 migrations (a 250-plant file in rounds of 100 with the refusals, the re-drop in rounds with a move in the first window and a plant gone from the last, the license hop), visual pass with the real files. Report SESSION_REPORTS/2026-09-11_sliced_mount.md. FLAGGED: the failed third mount's row may stand on the live mirror with the whole-file counts the old code wrote up front; it is history and the re-drop reconciles; which export it was is owed. The 1509 flowering export carries exactly 10,000 rows, a round number to check against METRC's grid before its gone marks are trusted. Wrangler, verbatim: "Your account has exceeded D1's free tier daily row write limit. Upgrade to a paid plan or wait until tomorrow (midnight UTC) to continue. See https://developers.cloudflare.com/d1/platform/limits/ for more details. [code: 7500]". The script restored wrangler.toml (sha256 879c101580b7e5c8, still naming 20260911), wrote DEPLOY/roll-result.json (ok false, step_failed migrations apply) and stopped; worker f186a48e never redeployed; the new database stands empty and unmigrated (the refused call was the first write). THE WALL: the account is on Workers Free; D1 free tier is 100,000 rows written per day and 5 million read, account wide, resetting at midnight UTC (8:00 PM Eastern), and every index on a written column adds a written row (metrc_plants: a TEXT primary key and two indexes, four writes per row; metrc_packages five). The afternoon's three sliced mounts (48,149 + 1,638 + 11,742 rows) were about 260,000 row writes; the counter caught up at the roll. Workers Paid has no daily cap (50 million rows written a month included, then $1.00 per million). The nine exports he sent are 109,678 METRC rows (about 450,000 row writes with indexes) plus 8,349 Zoho items: four and a half free-tier days for one load, and each daily plants re-drop would hit the wall again. SCRIBE'S RECOMMENDATION, sent to him: upgrade to Workers Paid before the beta (his account, his call; the same plan lifts the 10 ms CPU cap behind the 1102 of 2026-09-11). SHIPPED Scribe-direct: roll-beta.mjs learns THE RE-RUN LAW (step 3 asks wrangler d1 list --json for a database already named gripfactory-lite-grip-<today> and reuses it; a red migrations step names the 7500 wall in words and the already-exists case: delete the empty dated database by hand and run again); .gitignore gains db_backups/ (a full alpha dump inside a tracked tree is against G-9) and DROPS/; CLAUDE.md carries the 7500 sentence. THE LAW: 1. a finished lot (status drying, no open silo load) whose dry space is not a silo is attributed to the silo the HBN planned (harvest_batches.planned_dryspc, the Silo # lookup value, matched to the dry space named "Silo N") as long as its born package sits in the PNN barn (BARN); a weighing lot still fills only the silo it weighs into; 2. the barn-waiting state is his word, loading (green, still); filled is gone from the state set, the stylesheet and the strings; 3. a loading tile names the lots it waits for, each a link into Load Silo with the lot picked (/station/load_silo?pick=lot), with "in the barn" or "in the barn, planned here" under the lot number; 4. Load Silo preselects the planned silo when the lot's own space is not a silo; 5. /silos/live.json carries waiting per silo beside loads. BUILT Scribe-direct: src/db.ts (listSiloLotGrams joins the planned silo by name; BARN_WET_LOT_SELECT planned_silo_id), src/silo.ts (SiloState loading; SiloWaitingLot; siloWaitingLines), src/drying.ts, src/html.ts, src/i18n.ts (EN and ES), src/theme.ts x3; test/silo.test.ts, test/console.test.ts, test/drying.test.ts; scripts/smoke.mjs (the 220 acquire plans Silo 5; after the receive at PNN, Silo 5 reads loading on the strip, the tile names the lot with its link, live.json reads loading with 1500 wet grams, Load Silo preselects Silo 5, the console strip reads loading). No migration, no route change. WHAT WAS FOUND (Eastern): the wet pane bug of 0.30.84 hit the PNS tablet twice on the 15th before the fix reached the floor: 1:52 PM a spear lot of 35 totes (246,344 g) on 26011, finished in transit on truck 3; 3:47 PM the day's real PNS lot started on 26010 (748 totes, 5,561,556 g), moved to 26018 by the HBN pencil at 5:28 PM, finished 5:57 PM; 5:59 PM lot 19 on 26019 (753 totes, 5,414,572 g) finished 8:10 PM; 8:11 PM HBN 26020 minted and the Start button tapped four times in six seconds (the 0.30.89 bug), the crew weighing on until 9:23 PM (59 totes 422,094 g and 362 totes 2,927,160 g on two of the four); 7:46 AM the 16th three receipts at the PNN barn in three seconds, lot 19's on its virtual movement with its truck 5 manifest left open. Nothing of 18, 19 or 20 marked uploaded to METRC. THE LAW OF A RECORD REPAIR: one reviewed SQL file in app/scripts/repairs/ named by date and subject, written against a fresh snapshot (node scripts/db-snapshot.mjs pre_<label>), every UPDATE guarded by the value it expects and every log insert by NOT EXISTS on record, field and reason, so a second run changes nothing; ids deterministic; actor the owner's login; one reason prefix naming the date, the canon row and the record it follows; a fixed stamp; rehearsed on a local D1 loaded from the snapshot (both runs) and its floor effect read on a dev server; applied by his hands with wrangler d1 execute --remote --file, then a post snapshot, then the Scribe verifies the end state against the plan before the row folds. Voids keep the tote rows attached to the voided lot for the record. | 0.30.92 | 2026-09-16 | THE EFFECTIVE DATE (Scribe-direct under G-2). Two owner rulings 2026-09-16, minutes apart, verbatim: "We need to quickly add a date override in the load silo screen - PIN gated: 21319  Correcting for the issues from yesterday has made it impossible for me to load the silos at their proper time because the default date is today." and "Next move "Effective Date" = field on all transactional records - always pin gated - defaults to today and rarely needs to be touched." THE LAW: Effective Date is a field on a transactional record's form. It defaults to today (the farm's day, Eastern) and rarely needs to be touched: collapsed, one line reading Effective Date: YYYY-MM-DD (today). Opened, it offers the date (capped at today) and an Admin PIN box; any day other than today is honored only with the Admin PIN (app_config admin_pin) typed beside it, never a day ahead of today; blank, absent or today asks no PIN. The record's own instant falls on that day. Every write under another day leaves a change-log row with the reason effective date set under the Admin PIN (SYSTEM_REASON.effective_date), so the override is never silent. One field, one law: names effective_date and effective_pin, helper effectiveDateField and resolveEffectiveDate in src/effective.ts, the farm's clock in src/farmday.ts (etDate, etInstant, isCalendarDate, the console's helpers moved there and re-exported). LOAD SILO IS THE FIRST RECORD: the field sits under the Start time; another day requires the Start time (24h), which falls on the Effective Date and is the clock's zero (blank refused in words, not guessed); under the PIN any day back is allowed while the two-day floor of 0.30.9 stays for the crew's ordinary post; the browser's instant is taken when it agrees with the day, and without a script the farm's clock resolves the typed time; a time ahead of now still refuses. Refusals: 403 without the PIN or with another PIN (the Models PIN opens nothing here), 400 without a Start time, 400 ahead of today, 400 malformed, 400 an instant off the day. | 0.30.94 | 2026-09-16 | THE EFFECTIVE DATE PER STATION (Scribe-direct under G-2). Owner ruling 2026-09-16, verbatim: "Each station should have its own effective date field.  It's always the source data for the transaction date.  It defaults to (today).  It can be changed with a PIN" This generalizes THE EFFECTIVE DATE (0.30.92) from Load Silo to every station. His picks (AskUserQuestion): where a station already has a date field the Effective Date becomes that date (Trim's trim date, Cryo's cryo date), and Acquire HBN's harvest date stays free (it mints the HBN token; backdating there is routine). THE LAW: every station carries one Effective Date field, the source of that station's transaction date, today by default (the farm's day, Eastern), the Admin PIN (app_config admin_pin) for any other day, never ahead of today; the posted field is always effective_date with effective_pin beside it and the action writes the resolved date to the station's own column; every write under a non-today date carries a change-log marker (field effective_date, reason 'effective date set under the Admin PIN'), never silent; created_at stays the honest instant. Declarative: a pipeline FieldDef input:'effective_date' renders the effective.ts component and the action resolves it with resolveEffectiveDate; the synchronous renderer has no instant, so the date renders blank and the client fills today, its max and its default (a blank still resolves to today; the server is the gate). THE STATIONS: Wet Weight (the field on the Start lot header, the lot's date on wet_weight_entries.effective_date, a routine tap needs no touch); Unload (the field on the session line, the date on unload_sessions.effective_date, only a form carrying the value touches it so a weight-add never clobbers it); Trim (the old trim date IS the Effective Date, writes each package's trim_date, the Trimming console still buckets by it); Cryo (the old cryo date IS the Effective Date, writes cryo_date); Load Silo already carried it (0.30.92); Acquire HBN unchanged. BUILT: migration 0053 (effective_date on wet_weight_entries and unload_sessions; Trim, Cryo and Load Silo already store their date), src/effective.ts (the client-defaulted and stored-value renders, effectiveOverrideLog), src/pipeline.ts, src/html.ts, src/db.ts, src/actions.ts (the four actions; the dead entryDateOrToday retired), src/i18n.ts EN and ES; test/effective.test.ts (+3), test/trim.test.ts, test/surface.test.ts, test/gate.test.ts (the trim fixture posts effective_date plus the Admin PIN); the smoke's four station walks (the from-zero pin is 53; Trim and Cryo backdate under the PIN, a Cryo backdate without it is 403, the Wet Weight header carries the field and a backdate without the PIN is 403, the Unload session backdate under the PIN sticks and reads back and a weight-add does not clobber it). FOUND IN THE BUILD: the Wet Weight INSERT carried one extra placeholder after effective_date (16 ? for 15 binds, 20 tokens for 19 columns) and answered 500 on the first Start lot; found by reading the D1 path on the smoke's leftover database, fixed to 15; tsc could not see it (SQL is a string), the smoke did. Gates: tsc 0; vitest 30 files, 645 tests; from-zero smoke SMOKE PASS at 53 migrations; dash audit 0; visual pass in the three themes. | 0.30.96 | 2026-09-16 | THE PENDING QUEUE CLEAR (Scribe-direct under G-2). Owner ruling 2026-09-16, verbatim: "CAN WE REMOVE ALL THE DOCS that are parked on pending data uploads?" His picks (AskUserQuestion): the clear records the files as cleared, NOT uploaded to METRC (an honest disposition, never a false upload claim), and it is a reusable Clear all button on the page, not a one-time SQL. THE PICTURE: the queue was the whole harvest so far because nothing had ever been marked uploaded (metrc_uploads held two package_adjust marks); every finished batch, silo load, manifest and receipt pended. THE LAW: a Clear all pending control at the top of Pending Data Uploads, revealed behind a summary so a stray click cannot fire it, carrying the total across all seven lists (the whole /admin tree is behind the Admin PIN, so the clear is PIN-gated by that gate); one post disposes every pending file across the seven lists (wet weights, package files, receive manifests, manifests, splits, location changes, location moves) by inserting one metrc_uploads row per file with cleared = 1 and the change-log reason 'cleared, not uploaded to METRC', so the disposition is honest and never recorded as a METRC upload. It reuses the existing disposition path (the pending predicates already read any metrc_uploads row of a file's kind and ref as disposing it), so NO pending query changed; a future 'uploaded to METRC' report filters cleared = 0. No CSV is built (the bounded Worker room, 0.30.42): a cleared row needs only the kind, the batch and the ref the pending lists already carry, its filename a plain cleared_<kind>_<name>.csv. Reversibility matches per-item Mark uploaded: a time-based kind (wet weights, splits) re-pends on new activity, an existence kind stays cleared until its row is voided; the underlying harvest data is untouched, only clear-marks written. BUILT: migration 0054 (cleared column on metrc_uploads, default 0 so every existing mark stays a real upload), src/config.ts (SYSTEM_REASON.upload_cleared), src/actions.ts (clearAllUploads gathers the seven pending lists and inserts a cleared row per file, one db.batch), src/index.ts (POST /admin/uploads/clear-all, the ok=cleared flash), src/html.ts (the Clear all reveal and button with the total), src/i18n.ts EN and ES; test/surface.test.ts (the control renders with the summed count, none when empty); the smoke's clear-all walk (the from-zero pin is 54; with a pile pending the button carries a count, one post disposes every kind, the page reads Nothing pending, the flash says not-uploaded, the change log carries the reason). Gates: tsc 0; vitest 30 files, 646 tests; from-zero smoke SMOKE PASS at 54 migrations (Nothing pending after Clear all); dash audit 0; visual pass. THE GATE: paneFilter widens to trim_scheduled and cryo_scheduled; Trim Packages Entry lists only lots with a scheduled trim row, Cryo Weight Entry (and its bulk form) only packages with a scheduled cryo row; the Admin Data Queues and every export stay unfiltered. COMPLETION: automatic when the floor finishes the work (every lot_trimmed_unloaded path completes the trim row; setCryoWeight completes the cryo row) and manual by the arrow; demote logs and returns the item to scheduled (a trimmed lot does not reappear at Trim, a muted trimmed / cryo weighed word says why); unschedule soft-deletes the row under a system reason. EVERY SCHEDULING STATION CARRIES THE EFFECTIVE DATE (0.30.94) on the >> post. The existing trim and cryo smoke walks must schedule first (the ruling's ripple, named in the slice). SCRIBE ASSUMPTIONS, HIS WORD OWED (four forks recorded in the slice): the Status words (In queue on Trimming, Pasteurizing on Pasteurization, or Pasteurizing also on a Trimming lot whose packages moved on); unschedule with no reason and no PIN; the pages open like the other floor stations with only the demote PIN-gated; completion automatic as well as manual. | 0.30.104 | 2026-09-16 | THE ZONELESS ZERO FIXED, DEPLOY PENDING (worker only, his hands: npm run deploy:grip; no migration). His words on the 0.30.102 finding, verbatim: "well, how do I fix it?  If I wanted to go into it, would I use the wrong date?  Why wouldn't it just match the system timezone?" The Scribe's answer: the Worker keeps every instant in UTC and has no zone of its own; every screen converts to the browser's zone for display and every typed floor time (the Start time on Load Silo, the Effective Date) converts server-side in the farm's zone, Eastern, so a mis-set tablet cannot skew a record; the silo load pencil was the one field left taking the raw stored form and saving what was typed, unchecked. With the old box the only fix was to type the UTC clock four hours ahead with the Z, the date rolling after 8 PM Eastern, which is the trap itself, so the pencil was rebuilt instead. THE LAW AS BUILT (Scribe-direct under G-2): a stored instant a pencil takes is a FARM-TIME field (EditFieldDef.farmTime); it shows the stored instant as the Eastern wall clock (YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM) and takes the same shape back; the server parses it through farmInstantFromInput in src/farmday.ts (a zoneless wall clock is Eastern and resolves through etInstant, a space or a T between and seconds ignored; an instant carrying its zone, Z or an offset, stands as it is; any other shape refuses in words, Loaded at must be a farm-time date and time, YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM.; a blank refuses, Loaded at is required.; more than ten minutes ahead of now refuses, Loaded at is ahead of now.); nothing zoneless is ever stored again; old and new instants log under the typed reason. The silo load pencil is the first farm-time field, labeled Loaded at (farm time, YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM, the clock's zero); a zoneless value already stored shows in the box as the wall clock of the instant the Worker actually runs the clock from, so the box tells the truth about the zero. etWallClock(iso) renders any instant as the Eastern wall clock. The Load Silo form was never wrong (resolveLoadedAt and etInstant already stored the instant with its Z). His ruling, verbatim: "For cryo, replicate the trim line surface in cryo production reporting. His words at the forks, verbatim: what is consumed: "The containers created in trim constitute a total weight by product type.  That's what's consumed.  The containers made in trim are archival but no longer relevant to the process once they've been added to true METRC package IDs in cryo" and the ingredient: "They scan a tag that attaches to the weight and that weight is consumed from the sum of weight stored in the HBN.  The HBN continues to be important to the background data for METRC, but we use only the package ID after cryo to identify the material" His picks at the forks: a cryo line is a new output container; the paper waits for his template ("Hold for my template"), satisfied in the same window when he handed new_packages_autofill_template.t3.xlsx; a container trimmed after the run was scheduled waits for a new schedule; the outputs go straight to the Testing queue; the node keeps the name Cryo Weight Entry; Location Name always prints Cryo Room. A CORRECTION CARRIED IN THIS FOLD: the 0.30.109 row, _NOW and MEMORY said the trim room's entry pends the PROVISIONAL Package split file; reading the writer, that file belongs to the Marketplace's package splits (biz.ts, parent_item_id), not the trim room; Trim Packages Entry pends nothing when packages are entered, its containers being ledger rows beside the harvest package, and trim's only paper is a Package Adjustments row on the harvest package when the reconcile gate's Queue an adjustment CSV is chosen; the trim containers were never METRC packages, which is the owner's model exactly (the first true METRC package IDs after harvest are the ones cryo creates). THE LAW AS BUILT (Scribe-direct under G-2): (1) THE RUN: a pasteurization schedule row IS the run, one production_schedules row per HBN (stage cryo, wet_weight_entry_id and wip_package_id both null); the >> on the Pasteurization station makes the run and takes the HBN's free cryo-queue trim containers in as its inputs (cryo_run_inputs: id, run_id, wip_package_id, package_type_code, weight_grams as it stood, created_at); a container is free while it waits in the Cryo Queue, is not a cryo output and no live run holds it; an unscheduled run frees its containers; a container trimmed later shows the HBN on Available again for another >>. (2) THE COLLAPSE: the Pasteurization station's three panes read the HBN and its pool, never a container: Available reads n containers / X g in with the >>; Scheduled reads the run's identity (cryoRunIdentity: 3 containers / 22,679.00 g in / 1 out, 22,000.00 g) with Pasteurizing, cryo weighed once an output stands, the < and the >; Complete reads the same with the completion time and no demote; the rows scheduled per container under 0.30.97 fold into runs by migration 0058 (run-HBN-status, inputs in-<row id>, the per-container rows soft-deleted with the reason folded into the HBN run (canon 0.30.110)). (3) THE POOL: the run's inputs pool by product type; the working pane shows Type, In (g), Out (g), Remaining (g) per type and the totals (data-cryo-pool, data-pool-type, data-pool-remaining, data-pool-total-remaining); a line past a type's remaining, beyond half a cent, asks in words on the page and is never refused. (4) THE LINE: Cryo Weight Entry keeps its name and lists the scheduled runs by HBN in the left pane (queue-pane-item data-cryo-run, the HBN and n containers / X g), no containers; the picked run (GET /station/cryo_weight?pick=<run>) shows the pool, the Effective Date (today by default, the Admin PIN for another day), the Operator (prefilled from the last output) and the trim-style line: scan the new package tag, pick the type from the pool's types, type the weight, press + (POST /station/cryo_weight/run/:id/add); each line is a new container (wip_packages source cryo, cryo_run_id the run, package_code the tag, weight_grams and cryo_weight_grams its grams, cryo_date the Effective Date, cryo_operator, wet_weight_entry_id the first input's lot) born straight into the Testing queue with a ledger row (ITEM_KIND_WIP, metrc_tag the tag) at Cryo Room under the processor license AU-P-000694 on the landing farm; the tag must be a true METRC tag (1A plus 22 hex; the line IS the METRC package) and unused by any package or ledger row; the type must be in the pool; the weight above zero; the operator typed; refusals in words (CRYO_REFUSAL: The package tag must be 1A plus 22 hex characters. That package tag is already in use. That type is not in this run's pool. Operator is required.); the entered packages list beneath, newest first, with the reasoned pencil and void behind the PIN (an output's weight edit draws nothing from the harvest package). (5) FINISH RUN: one control (POST /station/cryo_weight/run/:id/finish), armed once a package stands (with none: Enter at least one package before finishing.): the trim containers the run took in move cryo_queue to pasteurized (transition cryo_consumed; archival, out of every queue and off the live inventory sheet) and their ledger grams go to zero under the reason consumed by the cryo run; the container is archival; the run completes; the Pasteurization station's > on a run is the same Finish run; a run with outputs cannot be unscheduled (A run with outputs cannot be unscheduled; finish it.); a finished run cannot be demoted (A finished run cannot be demoted; its outputs stand.). (6) THE NEW PACKAGES FILE: a finished run with outputs pends New Packages on Pending Data Uploads as new_packages_<HBN>.xlsx in the owner's T3 autofill template shape: its three header rows (the repeater row of line in repeaterLines and ingredient in line.Ingredients, the field paths line.TagId through ingredient.FinishDate, the labels Tag Number, Location Name, Item Name, Same Item, Quantity, Unit Of Measure, Note, Actual Date, Package Number, Ingredient Quantity, Ingredient Unit Of Measure, Finish Package, Ingredient Finish Date) on the sheet named new_packages_autofill_template. then one row per output: Tag Number the scanned tag, Location Name Cryo Room, Same Item yes, Quantity in Grams as a plain number, Actual Date the line's cryo date as text, and the ingredient Package Number the HBN's harvest package tag (the ledger row born at Finish lot, its tag or its code) with Ingredient Quantity the output's weight in Grams; an HBN with two lots draws from each lot's package in turn, a package's share being the grams its containers put into the run (allocateIngredients, lot order); an output that straddles two lots carries a second ingredient row with the line columns blank; an over issue folds into the last package; Finish Package and Ingredient Finish Date stay blank until his word; the route /admin/uploads/new_packages/<run>.xlsx and its mark uploaded (metrc_uploads kind new_packages, ref_id the run); Clear all includes it. (7) THE SHEET AND THE TAT: the Inventory sheet's live packages exclude pasteurized containers (their grams live on in the outputs); the Testing queue and its representative weight read the outputs; the TAT trim-to-cryo span for an output runs from the earliest trim container the run took in to the output's birth. The cryo_weights widget, setCryoWeight and the per-package completion are retired; xlsx.ts sheets may name themselves, write plain numbers and leave blank cells truly blank. His rulings on the live cryo run, verbatim and in order: "in cryo weight entry, add the product categories: 1. WPR 2. BUD 3. SHA" (his numbered list, one per line); "Cryo weights should be listed beneath the entry surface (just like trim), the plus line should ratify what's in the entry fields and reset the fields to null.  Keep the running package total and how much is left in the parent at the top (same as trim )"; then of the totals "sorry, looks like totals are already correct"; and "When a hard entered (under the entry surface) tag number is scanned into the entry surface a warning (in the language selected at the pill) warns the user of a duplicate tag and doesn't allow it to proceed." His pick at the one fork put to him (the New Packages file's Item Name and Same Item per category): Same Item yes, as built. THE LAW AS BUILT (Scribe-direct under G-2): (1) THE CATEGORIES: a cryo line is entered under a product category, not a trim package type; the categories are a value in data (L-4), app_config cryo_product_categories seeded WPR,BUD,SHA by migration 0059 in his order, editable in Admin, Lookups, app_config, the same three when blank (CONFIG_KEYS.cryo_product_categories, parseCryoCategories, listCryoCategories); the line's Category box lists them in that order with a blank first choice and requires a pick; the server refuses a blank (Pick the product category.) and a code off the list (That product category is not on the cryo list.), case folded on the way in; the category is stored as the output package's type code, so the Testing queue, the entered list, the Inventory sheet and the ledger read WPR, BUD or SHA on the new package; only is_bud trim types reach the Cryo Queue, so the pool is bud trim and a line of any category draws from the run's pool as a whole (the per-type Out of 0.30.110 is gone). (2) THE POOL AT THE TOP: the pool table stays first in the working pane and keeps his totals: in by trim type on the left, out by product category on the right, row for row (data-pool-type and data-pool-in, data-out-category and data-out-grams); a Total under each side; Remaining as the last line (data-pool-total-in, data-pool-total-out, data-pool-total-remaining on the table); the over-issue line reads the pool's total remaining and past half a cent asks in words (This weight over issues the pool. Record it anyway?), never refuses. (3) THE PLUS: the plus posts the line and the page comes back with the tag, the category and the weight blank; the Operator stays filled from the last line; the entered packages list beneath the entry surface, newest first, pencil and void behind the PIN, a Category column in place of Type. (2) A VOIDED LOT NEVER OCCUPIES: listOpenSiloLoads and getOpenSiloLoadForSilo ignore a load whose lot is voided (w.deleted_at IS NULL), so an orphan can never hold a silo again and the Silo 1 card clears on this deploy before the repair closes its row. | 0.30.124 | 2026-09-18 | THE MANIFEST BUILDER AND THE SECURITY TALK, LOGGED, TALKING (forks to him on the question tool; nothing built). Owner ruling 2026-09-18, verbatim (his line breaks folded to spaces; the full text with its breaks lives in the 0.30.125 report when the build lands): "New interco logistics layout: Three equal panes Left Pane: Origin License: selecting a license exposes the METRC packages ordered numerically based on the last six digits At the top of the exposed list, a "last six" filter (user types the last six digits and it focuses on all the packages that meet the criterea. Add a package to the far right pane by clicking ">>" next to it Middle Pane: Destination License: Selecting exposes METRC location within that license (we call this "receive into") Right Pane: Header: all the herbs and spices needed for a METRC manifest (use our library of templates--I believe this one is T3 based) Underneath the header, packages added from the left each with its own "<<" proceeding it that "puts the package back in" the origin license list. One more thing: let's have a security discussion.  I have varying degrees of email access on the team and the users are regularly needing my management team to sign in for them.  I'd like to make the cloudflare login keyed to a cell phone number instead of an email address.  I'd like to extend the code request window longer as well, maybe a week. [finish] button [cancel] button". THE SCRIBE'S READING BEFORE THE FORKS: the surface is the blank Interco Logistics tab (0.30.108) reborn as one page of three equal panes; the packages are the METRC mirror's rows for the picked origin license (metrc_packages, gone_at null), sorted by the number in the tag's last six digits, the filter matching the typed digits against those six; the destination's locations are the license's inventory_location rows (0.30.40's seed, license_value the license or null at its farm) and the mirror's own location names for that license; the header is the T3 Licensed Transfers block already vendored (prompts/2026-09-03_t3_manifest_template_blank.csv; T3ManifestHeader in src/interco.ts: recipient from the destination license, planned route, transfer type, invoice, payment terms, departure, arrival, gross weight and unit, transporter license and phone, driver name and two licenses, vehicle make, model, plate), prefilled from the newest finished manifest on the same origin and destination pair; >> and << move a row between the panes in the browser, the filter too, nothing written until [finish]; [finish] writes one interco_movements row (completed; destination_location the receive-into pick) with one item per tag (migration 0061 adds interco_movement_items.metrc_tag; the mirror's tag is the package.Id) and the T3 manifest CSV pends on Pending Data Uploads, the one export home; [cancel] discards the unsaved build. THE FORKS ON THE TOOL: what [finish] produces; the receive-into source; packages already on a finished manifest; the per-package wholesale price. THE SECURITY FACTS (Cloudflare's docs, read today): Access one-time PIN is email only ("Cloudflare Access can send a one-time PIN (OTP) to approved email addresses"; the code "expires 10 minutes after the initial request"; SMS delivery is an open community request, not supported or planned); the code window he wants is the SESSION, not the code: the application's Session Duration and the global session duration each run "between 15 minutes and one month" (default 24 hours) and the global one wins when it lapses, so a week (or a month) of no re-login is a dashboard setting today, no IdP change; a phone-keyed login needs an external IdP (Auth0 passwordless SMS through Twilio or a custom phone provider, identified by phone number, code three minutes) wired to Access as a generic OIDC provider, whose Email claim name Access lets him choose, so the phone (or a synthetic address the IdP mints from it) rides Cf-Access-Authenticated-User-Email and the app's actor law needs a people row keyed to that value (a small slice: people.phone or the synthetic address as the row's email). The Scribe's recommendation: set the week now on email, give the team members who lack mail a company address or alias, and take the phone IdP only if the week does not end the sign-in-for-them calls. |

THE LAW AS BUILT (Scribe-direct under G-2): 1. THE THREE PANES: the Interco Logistics tile opens one form of three equal cards, Origin license, Destination license, Manifest; before any pick every list reads - EMPTY - (0.27.6); every old path under /interco and /virtual-move still lands here. 2. THE ORIGIN: the License select carries the license list (Admin, Lookups, license); picking one reloads the page with the METRC mirror's live packages under it, one row each (the last six bold, the whole tag muted beneath, the mirror's item, quantity and location), sorted by the number in the last six ascending, a tail that is not six digits after them by its text, ties by the whole tag; the Last six box atop the list matches the typed digits anywhere in the last six as they are typed (more than six characters match the whole tag), the count line reads the rows shown, no match reads - EMPTY -; each live row carries >>. 3. THE DESTINATION: the License select carries every license but the origin; the pick fills Receive into with the location names the mirror's packages carry under that license (live rows; a license whose packages have all gone keeps the names its rows ever carried), A to Z, required when names exist, - EMPTY - when none, and sets the Recipient line; the destination pick rides the origin reload. 4. THE MANIFEST: the Header is the vendored T3 Licensed Transfers block (T3ManifestHeader: planned route, transfer type, invoice, payment terms, departure, arrival, gross weight and unit, transporter license and phone, driver name and two licenses, vehicle make, model, plate; the recipient derived from the destination), prefilled from the newest finished manifest on the same origin and destination pair (a typed field never overwritten); >> moves a row under the header where it reads << and gains a Wholesale price box; << puts it back in last-six order and clears the price; nothing is written until [finish]; [cancel] is the bare page. 5. [FINISH]: one interco_movements row finished as it lands (interco, completed, the farms from the license list, destination_location the receive-into pick, the header as typed with CRLF folded to LF), one interco_movement_items row per package with the METRC tag, the mirror's quantity and unit and the price (local_inventory_item_id empty: the package is the mirror's), every field in record_change_log; the page returns with the flash, a banner naming the manifest's short id, count and a Download the T3 file link, and the file downloading on its own (/interco/manifest/:id/csv under the interco gate); the same CSV pends on Pending Data Uploads, downloads and marks there; the move stands in Admin, Data Queues, Logistics Moves with the reasoned void, which frees its tags and drops the file off the export home. 6. THE T3 FILE: the three template header rows, the first data row with the recipient, the header and the first package, each further package its own row; package.Id the tag, package.WholesalePrice the price typed (blank when none), the gross weight columns blank; manifest_<short id>_<yyyymmdd>.csv. 7. DIM, NO >>: a package riding a finished, unvoided manifest out of the origin reads dim with "on manifest <short id>" and no >>; the finish refuses it by name. 8. THE TIE-COMPLETE PAGE, a correction of 0.30.137's poll law: 1. the page asks for one row past the limit to learn whether more follow; 2. when that row shares the last kept row's change instant, every row of that instant beyond the limit is fetched under the same clauses (since, live, site; by id) and appended, so a page never ends inside a tie and the limit is a floor exceeded only by the rows sharing its last instant; 3. next_since stays the page's last instant and since stays strictly after it, so nothing is lost and nothing repeats between pages; more reads whether rows remained past the limit, not the count against the limit; 4. nothing changes for the poller (same envelope, same cursor, same limit; a page may run over by a few rows). THE FEED NAMES THE REAL STRAIN (his pick): lot.strain is the batch's strain as Lookups names it, the one a METRC tag prints; lot.market_strain rides beside it, the typed market label, else the strain's own name. THE PICTURE: each shift card of the Harvest Console's Trimming section carries his picture in place of the waffle (TRIM circle, T-0, SORTER, MARVEL, BIO tilted, BUD, RETRIM, his layout, our panel and line colors through CSS); six flow bars of his width (44 of 1000) carry the continuum tubes' green candy stripes (#43c07a on #2a7d4f, 7 wide at 135 degrees on 20 by 20 tiles) moving toward the next machine at the drying locations' calm cadence, 900 ms a tile, only while the line runs; lines drop from the three endpoints, meet, and hang the shift total (lb and g); 2. THE FIGURES ON THE MACHINES: BIO the BIO stream; BUD the Total Bud with bulk and prepack under it; RETRIM the rest of Other (TRIM, the WIP left, every other type); T-0 the grams weighed and the package count; the TRIM circle the typed input pounds; 3. HIS MATH in his order under the picture: Other = TRIM + BIO + WIP left (+ MIX, KIEF, WASTE and any other type, so the total stays honest); Bulk Bud = SHAKE + BUD; Prepack Bud = WPR, SMALLS by his 0.30.88 pick, read as the BTR-set types whose cryo category is WPR (app_config cryo_type_categories); Total Bud = Bulk + Prepack (app_config btr_type_codes); BTR = Total Bud over the shift total; then the weight ran, the DLH box, Lbs/DLH; 4. THE WIP LEFT: a lot finished on the trim line within the day's window (its status log to trimmed, by Remove from queue or the adjustment choice) with dry weight above the grams its trim containers recorded leaves the difference as WIP on Other for the shift whose operator cut its last package; the shift total is weighed plus WIP left, so Total Bud + Other equals it; a lot finished over or without a dry weight leaves nothing; 5. THE HOURS FROM THE SHEET: under the lines, the day's Cutting and Bucking hours per farm (PNN, ARL, PNS; harvest_labor_hours), none where nothing stands; 6. INPUT LBS: under the hours, one box per shift, total lbs run through T-0, saved to trim_line_inputs (migration 0065: id, work_date, shift, lbs, stamps, UNIQUE work_date and shift) at /harvest/trim-input, two decimals, a blank clearing, logged under the reason input lbs typed, the flash Input lbs saved., the picked day riding home; 7. RUNNING: a package weighed at the trim weight entry node in the last 30 minutes (entry instant, cryo outputs aside) lights the card of its operator's shift (the word RUNNING pulsing in the card head at the silo throb's 1.6 s, slowed under reduced motion, never stilled; the stripes moving); a recent package by an operator on neither shift lights a RUNNING note under the cards; the flash reads the present whatever day the console shows. FIXED ON THE WAY: the day's trim packages no longer count cryo outputs (source cryo), which carried no operator and read as unassigned grams. THE LAW: (1) the Scribe never commits to app/ with force; every commit carries the mtime guard from the staging that preceded it, and a refusal means another hand has been in the file: stage, read, merge, then write; (2) before any commit under app/ the Scribe stages the device's copy and compares it to the sha it last committed or gated; drift means a build session has been there: read SESSION_REPORTS/ for a report newer than the last gated one, gate it, then merge; (3) one hand in app/ at a time: while a brief is out, the Scribe's own Scribe-direct work waits, or is built on a fresh stage taken after the build's report lands, never on copies taken before the brief went out; (4) before a brief goes out, the Scribe's hands are out of app/ (everything it holds committed and gated), and while a build session holds the tree the Scribe's writes to app/ stop entirely, guard or no guard; (5) a build session hands its report in chat when it seals it, and the Scribe reads SESSION_REPORTS/ before it builds anything.
