| 0.30.178 | 2026-09-22 | THE FIRST TIME PASS RATE IS BY WEIGHT OF THE PARENT PACKAGE, and THE FIFTY POUNDS IS FOR NON-INFUSED PRODUCT (a ruling on the Testing section's meaning; brief C Part 2b dispatched; Part 2's worker-only block stands as handed). Owner, 2026-09-22, verbatim, quoting the Scribe's eight Part 2 assumptions: "What do you mean by this?  This one is usually pretty tough to explain to people, let alone a machine.  The record shows the parent tag and the HBN for a reason.  The tag that gets used for a test sample is irrelevant, what's important is the batch it represents which is the parent tag ID.  You mentioned 50 lbs, and this is only kind of true.  50lbs and down for representative tests of non-infused product.  There is no limit on infused product.  That's not important right now, but it will be when we get into processing.  What's important here is, what we're really asking is: did this 50lbs only get one full compliance panel done before it became "passed"?  And, of my total weight of representative 50lb and under packages, what percentage (by total weight of the parent packages) only needed to be tested once?" HIS PICKS on the question tool: the weight of a parent package is "The batch weight at its first full panel" (the order's batch_grams; offered: the parent's live METRC weight; the lot's dry weight); the denominator is "Every representative package with a full-panel result" (a first fail counts as not once from that moment, whether or not the retest has happened; pending panels, potency-only and R&D orders stay out; offered: only packages at a final pass; every package ever scheduled); the lane is "Deploy part 2 now, fix the rate as part 2b before part 3" (offered: hold the deploy; Scribe-direct). THE LAW: THE UNIT IS THE PARENT PACKAGE, the batch the sample represents, named by its parent tag (test_orders.source_package_tag when tag-shaped, else source_package_code, else the lot); every full compliance order on one parent is one testing history in created_at order; the sample tag is a manifest detail and never a key in the Testing section. THE FIRST FULL PANEL: a parent enters the rate when its earliest panel = full_compliance order has a sample with overall_passed set; passed means it needed one panel, failed means it did not, from that moment, whatever a retest or a remediation does later; potency and rnd orders are never a panel for this rate; a parent with no resulted full panel is not in it. THE WEIGHT is that first order's batch_grams; THE RATE is the weight of the parents that needed one panel over the weight of every parent in the rate, shown to one decimal with the pounds (at 448, two decimals) and the package counts under it; an empty season reads no results. BY LAB (the lab of the first full panel) then FAILURE TYPE (the failed test types of that panel, by weight and count, a two-type fail under both); BY STRAIN (the HBN's) then FARM; the COA and THC lists stay keyed on the parent tag and gain the attempt beside the date. THE FIFTY POUNDS: the 50 lb batch limit (22,400 g at 448) is for representative samples of non-infused product; infused product has no batch limit; the desk's refusal over 22,400 g stands for harvest lots today and becomes type-aware when processing brings infused product to the desk, which then gets its own line outside this rate. WHAT THIS SUPERSEDES: of Part 2's eight assumptions (0.30.177) the rate-by-sample-count ones (the rate on attempt-1 samples with overall_passed; a two-type fail counted once in the rate and once per type by count; the lists carrying later attempts while the count-rate did not; one decimal and the hundredth) yield to this row; distinct lots in the three counts, retest_scheduled not scheduled, waiting by created_at, and the parent tag as the list key stand, the last affirmed by his words. | 0.30.183 | 2026-09-22 | THE DRYING TOTALS AND THE STRAIN ON THE LOT LINE, DEPLOY PENDING, WORKER ONLY (his hands: cd "C:\Users\DennisWallace\Documents\Claude\Projects\gripfactory Practical\app" then npm run deploy:grip). Scribe-direct under G-2 on the quiet tree of 0.30.182 (Part 2b gated and LIVE; Part 3 waiting on his continue), one hand in app/ (0.30.165). Owner, 2026-09-22, verbatim: "Some small things:  Under the dry spaces on the harvest console (harvest), Total in Dry Spaces, Total in Cure Spaces, Total Dry Queued  Under the lots in the dry spaces, show the market strain (or the actual strain if the market strain is null--should already be the rule)"; on the question tool, free text: "Loaded in dry rooms and silos only - also add a dry weight estimate based on an Admin lookup (default to 21%)". THE LAW: (1) THE DRYING TOTALS, one line under the dry space tiles in the console's Drying section (/harvest), three figures, each the pounds at 448 and the whole grams with the lot count under. Total in Dry Spaces is the wet weight of the open loads in the silos and the dry rooms only (the freezer's chilling material and the lots still waiting to load stay out), and under it the dry estimate, est. dry X lb at P percent, the loaded grams times the Admin percent (app_config projected_dry_yield_pct, the same number the tiles' of expected reads; 22 by default and 22 on his floor since 0.30.77; his default to 21 is the number to set there, so the two surfaces never disagree). Total in Cure Spaces is the dry weight of the lots on CSA and CSB. Total Dry Queued is the Unloaded Queue for trim (unloaded lots with a live trim schedule, the queue the trimming clocks read), its dry grams. (2) THE STRAIN ON THE LOT LINE: every lot line on a dry space tile, a loaded lot with its clock or a lot waiting to load, carries the strain under the lot number, the market strain when the HBN names one, else the strain's name (the 0.30.149 rule, COALESCE(b.market_strain, s.name)), on every surface that draws the strip; a lot whose HBN names neither carries no line. (3) Spanish speaks: Total en espacios de secado, Total en espacios de curado, Total seco en cola, seco est. X lb al P por ciento, N lotes. | 0.30.196 | 2026-09-23 | THE PREPACK SCHEDULER BY PACKAGE, THE HBN INHERITED (a ruling; brief G dispatched). Owner, 2026-09-23, verbatim: "I'm not sure the production scheduling surface for prepack makes sense to me.  If I adopt a package to an HBN does it conflate that package ID to the HBN I adopted?  Or is that a filter?  HBNs can't be changed, they live with every package and are inherited generationally.  A filter at the top of the list for HBN or last 6 would be useful, but the scheduler should only really be scheduling by the package ID (or the last six digits of it).  Use the ">>"/"<<" system we've precedented throughout the app." THE SCRIBE'S ANSWER: today's Adopt writes the picked HBN onto the package (its lot, its ledger item, its schedule row), a binding and not a filter; and the prepack Available pane's >> inherited the trim pattern, scheduling every passed WPR of an HBN at once (scheduleHbn). Both go. HIS PICK on the question tool, for the ten strangers whose METRC harvest the app never minted (the 46xxx names of 0.30.194): Mint the HBN from METRC (adoption creates the harvest batch from METRC's harvest name exactly as METRC states it, marked born from METRC, no lot weighed here; the package inherits it; no pick anywhere; every stranger with a Source Harvest is adoptable). THE BRIEF: BRIEFS/2026-09-23_prepack_scheduler_by_package.md (10,965 bytes, sha256 4f1adbd46c7d776c), seven laws: THE PACKAGE CARRIES ITS HBN (wip_packages.harvest_batch_id by a 0073 rebuild that also makes wet_weight_entry_id nullable, backfilled from every row's lot, indexed; every writer sets it; the prepack feed, the scheduled and complete lists, the station's job, the pending files and the console's section read the HBN from the package with the lot LEFT JOINed; the other stages keep their lots); THE SCHEDULER BY PACKAGE (the Prepack page lists packages on all three panes, the last six first, then the strain, the grams and the HBN muted; >> on each Available row schedules that package alone through a new schedulePackage posting wip_package_id; << unschedules; the arrow opens the station; the Available count is packages; Trimming and Pasteurization keep their HBN groups and their >> per HBN); THE FILTER (a text box at the top, HBN or last six, narrowing the three panes and From METRC as typed, the value riding ?q= and the server matching it too; the filter never changes what is scheduled); THE HBN IS INHERITED, NEVER PICKED (From METRC loses its select; each row shows the HBN it will inherit from METRC's Source Harvest or one source package's, known or new from METRC, or no harvest in METRC's lineage with no Adopt; Adopt posts the tag alone; a new HBN is MINTED from METRC's name exactly as stated, the seven segments read through parseHbn, farm, grow method, manicure, the serial and year from the seventh segment, the entry date from the token else the packaged date else the farm day, the planned dry space from the sixth, the strain by name else minted from METRC's name with the fourth segment as its code, the license from the mirror, born_from metrc, a new harvest_batches column; a name that is not seven segments or whose year and serial belong to another HBN refuses in words and mints nothing; a METRC-born batch has no lot, no plants and no wet weight); THE METRC-BORN HBN STAYS OUT OF THE HARVEST (every surface that counts or lists the farm's harvest excludes born_from metrc or is proven unaffected, listed one line each in the report); Spanish; nothing else moves (the mirror release untouched; the station, Finish Pack, the carry and the console section untouched beyond reading the HBN from the package). THE FLOOR MEANWHILE: no adoption until G lands (today's binds the pick; the ten cannot be adopted at all); the mirror release and the hand Release stand. His words, verbatim, with one screenshot of the Prepack scheduling page LIVE in the four lines (two paragraphs folded to one line at the paragraph break): "always include commas. THREE RULINGS: (1) COMMAS ALWAYS: the house grams style 8,505 g stands (the first owed word of 0.30.199 answered). (2) THE HBN IS THE REAL STRAIN: the market strain names the product (METRC's strain is the market strain, and 0.30.9's inheritance, strain when the market strain is null, is the product rule), but the HBN's fourth segment is the real strain, so a batch minted from METRC's name takes the live strain whose hbn_code is that segment and carries METRC's name as market_strain; an adoption never mints, renames or name-matches a strain (strainForMetrcBatch goes); a code no live strain carries, or two carry, refuses in words naming the code and Admin, Lookups, Strains, inserting nothing; the adopted package's ledger item carries the batch's strain on both paths; a known batch is never rewritten. Why it bites, read on the floor's strains (snapshot 20260922-080023, 96 live, no code carried twice): 005 Apple Fritter, 062 Super Boof, 078 Blue Andeez, and Heir Heads at 077; his screenshot read Heir Heads - 8,505 g under 46258-PNN-ID-062-0-NA-26007 and Super Boof - 20,639 g under 46248-PNN-ID-062-1-NA-26002, so today's name match would have hung a 062 harvest on the 077 strain, and a market name the app never heard would have minted a strain under a taken code (the minted-strain question of 0.30.197 answered with it). (3) >> NOT ADOPT: the From METRC row's control is the >> of the scheduler. HIS PICK on the question tool, verbatim: "Adopt and schedule in one >> (Recommended)": >> on a From METRC row is a button of the Available pane's shared schedule form (name=tag) so the effective date and q ride with it; adoptFromMetrc mints the batch when new, writes the package, its ledger item and one prepack schedule row in one db.batch; ok=adopted&n=1 flashes Adopted and scheduled 1.; the package lands in Scheduled with << and >; << unschedules it into Available as an app package (no un-adopt); the Adopt button, the per-row form and the /prepack/adopt route go. No adoption had been made on the floor (every row read new from METRC); HE HOLDS THE ADOPTIONS UNTIL H IS LIVE. (3) THE HISTORICAL LOTS ARE MINTED FROM THE SHEET: a lot the app lacks (no string, no year and serial) mints with the sheet's date, farm, grow method by word checked against the third segment, strain BY NAME (the sheet is reality), market strain through inheritMarketStrain, manicure, the first row's tag, serial and year from the seventh segment, planned_dryspc from the sixth, the farm's grow license, status open, born_from sheet (a third HARVEST_BORN value; only metrc is excluded from harvest surfaces), logged under harvest details (sync); METRC's name for the same year and serial (the mirror's source_harvests through hbnFromMetrcName) wins the string when it differs, the sheet's string kept on the row, so 26004 mints as 46254-PNN-ID-005-1-NA-26004 with 08-24, Apple Fritter, manicure No and 26007 as 46258-PNN-ID-062-0-NA-26007 with Heir Heads, manicure Yes, 08-24, and 26009 as Banana Berry Wine by name; refusals (nothing minted, named in the message): an unknown strain, a year not the date's, an unknown farm or grow method, a serial another HBN holds. (4) THE APP'S OWN BATCHES ARE RECONCILED: the sheet's market strain and manicure replace the app's when they differ, the tag fills a blank, one log row each with the old value; THE HBN FOLLOWS METRC: the app's hbn becomes METRC's text for the same year and serial, logged as HBN follows METRC (0.30.203); the sheet's differing string never renames; the report lists every table storing the HBN text and follows it or says why not; a rename that would break a pending file refuses; entry_date, farm, grow method and strain of an app-minted batch are never changed by the sync (a disagreement is named for the owner). (6) THE SOURCE TODAY IS AN UPLOAD: Import Data accepts the workbook as .xlsm (a new kind at the XLSB_MAX_BYTES cap), lands both tabs on the spot as one sheet_sync_runs row with trigger upload and the flash Harvest Details: {seen} seen, {landed} landed, {changed} changed, {skipped} skipped; Labor: {seen} seen, {landed} landed; bytes not stored; .xlsx still queues; the Graph pull runs both tabs once the Entra ids are lit. (8) THE WORDS in Spanish. (9) NOTHING ELSE MOVES. | 0.30.220 | 2026-09-24 | THE UNFINISH: RULED, BRIEF K WRITTEN AND DISPATCHED. His words, verbatim: "I need to be able to unfinish a package in production scheduling surfaces and have it also remove the next-in-line queue record from the next process when fired". HIS PICKS on the question tool, verbatim: the surfaces "Trim, Pastuerization, Prepack, AND we need a way to unfinish things that pasteurization finishes"; the guard "Refuse, name what was worked (Recommended)"; the gate "Admin PIN, like Demote (Recommended)"; the things pasteurization finishes "Both, one at a time (Recommended)". WHAT THE SCRIBE READ: the three scheduling surfaces carry Available, Working and Complete panes; the step back today is Demote with the Admin PIN on trim alone (the row complete to scheduled, one log row), cryo and prepack refusing; trim Complete flips the schedule row alone (containers are born at Trim Packages Entry into the Cryo Queue by type); pasteurization's >> makes one run per HBN taking the free containers as cryo_run_inputs, lines at Cryo Weight Entry bear outputs at entry into the Testing Queue, Finish run consumes the inputs (cryo_queue to pasteurized, the child ledger item to 0 g) and completes the run; prepack's Finish Pack zeroes the parent's grams, opens a carry when pushed, sets the parent packed and the job complete (the FG items born per line); the Testing Queue's generic Demote would step a cryo-born output to a queue it never came from. The floor at the post snapshot of 14:46Z: trim 13 complete and 2 working, pasteurization 13 complete and 2 working, no prepack jobs; 465 pasteurized, 212 in the Cryo Queue, 115 in the Testing Queue, 29 released. THE LAW (BRIEFS/2026-09-24_the_unfinish.md, 11,442, 062d646b4fc1613b; nine laws): ONE WORD, ONE CONTROL (Unfinish with the Admin PIN on every Complete pane, POST /production-scheduling/<stage>/unfinish/<id>, the row complete to scheduled under SYSTEM_REASON.schedule_unfinish; the scheduling surfaces' Demote retires, the Data Queues' Demote stays); CHECK EVERYTHING, THEN WRITE ONCE (every downstream record read first, a worked one refusing the whole act by name, nothing written on a refusal, one db.batch on success); TRIM (an empty scheduled run on the HBN unscheduled as Unschedule does it, a run with outputs or a finished run refusing by name); PASTEURIZATION, THE RUN (complete to scheduled; every consumed container pasteurized to cryo_queue by the new transition cryo_unconsumed with its child item's grams restored; every output voided with its ledger item; refusals by name on a released, packed, gone or sampled output, a marked test_sample or new_packages upload, a container's item moved since); PASTEURIZATION, ONE AT A TIME (on the finished run's page each consumed container Unfinish: back to the Cryo Queue with its grams, out of the pool, refused while the New Packages file is marked; each output Unfinish in place of the void link: output and item voided, grams back to the pool, refused when released, packed, gone, sampled or in a marked upload; the Testing Queue's Demote on a source cryo package becomes the output unfinish); PREPACK (job complete to scheduled, the parent packed to released by the new transition prepack_unpacked with its grams restored from the finish's own log row, an untouched carry voided, a drawn carry or a marked package_finish or new_packages file refusing by name; the lines and their FG items stand); THE WORDS in Spanish; THE TESTS (each surface's unfinish and each refusal, the wrong PIN; the smoke walking a run unfinish, a container unfinish and a trim unfinish with an empty run); NOTHING ELSE MOVES. No migration expected (0075 only if a column proves necessary, said in the report). Committed (path verified absent), identical both sides, remotes 0; the paste handed; the tree his until the hand-back (0.30.165; device tree 45b21f74675c2f47). |

HIS ANSWERS on the question tool, verbatim. Where the split happens: "per tote at weigh-in, but the last HBN is the default on the next one, that way when the user references a different lot on the truck, they don't have to do it per record, but once when they switch it over it stays unless they change it again." What the surface shows: "the surface will show header data like it does now, but a line on the record that shows minted HBNs, the last one selected is the default.  The first one on the list is always the one that is selected on the header". Patrick's schedule: "Yes, in the trucks brief". The second round: mixed HBNs "Every entry, dry pro rata (Recommended)"; the load "Shares to any space"; the schedule's shape "Three surfaces by space kind"; the package "One package per HBN (Recommended)". WHAT THE CODE SAYS (the Scribe's survey): THE BLANK SLATE (0.30.108) retired the trucks, so no truck is in the app at all: createWetWeight refuses a truck pick, Move To is a PNN silo or hang room for every farm, truck_number is written null and the header does not render it, finishLot cuts no manifest, every package is born in PNN's BARN, and the road and arrivals surfaces are unrouted (receiveTruckLot, roadChips, listInboundTruckLots, listInboundMovements, intercoReceivingPage have no caller). THE MESS: one lot per HBN per truck started and finished by hand, and an HBN leaving the pick pane the moment one of its lots is finished with no weighing lot left (NO_FINISHED_LOT_SQL, the 2026-08-28 ruling), so the next truck of the same HBN cannot be started unless it was started first. THE LAW AS BRIEFED, PART ONE, THE TRUCK RECORD: Truck # returns to the wet weight header (free text, optional, prefilled with the farm day's next number; Move To stays THE BLANK SLATE's); migration 0075 adds wet_weights.harvest_batch_id (null reads as the lot's HBN); every tote carries an HBN select of the minted HBNs (the header's farm's open batches, the header's first and selected until another is chosen, the last selected the default for the next tote), stored and logged by addLotWeight, shown on the row when it differs, summed per HBN on the panel, pencilled with a reason while the lot is weighing; the pick pane keeps a batch finished today (farm day) and hides one finished before today; Finish lot SPLITS the truck into one lot per HBN (the header lot keeps its HBN's totes; each other HBN gets a copy of the header with its totes moved, one log row each under SYSTEM_REASON.truck_split, every lot finished and one package minted per lot as today, so one package per HBN is born and nothing after the finish learns a new shape), the plant count and the METRC tag asked per HBN on the finish form, the code shortLotId with -T<n> when the lot carries a truck, the flash naming every lot born of the split. PART TWO, THE DRYING SCHEDULE: SCHEDULE_STAGES gains silos, dry_rooms and freezers, each a station-triple like the lines: Available the lots Load Silo lists (grouped by HBN, the truck, the wet grams, the planned space; >> opens Load Silo's form limited to this kind's spaces, any lot to any space), Working the open loads under each space (the clock, Start and Pause, > to Unload Dry Space), Complete the kind's lots unloaded in the last seven days (the dry, the yield); views over wet_weight_entries, silo_loads, unload_sessions and the spaces, no new table; THE JOINT UNLOAD on a space holding several open loads: one form, the total dry and the unload's questions once (the gate's choice included), each lot's share pro rata by wet grams (whole grams, the remainder on the last), then each lot's finishUnload as today.
